financial-analysis--straight-line-depreciation-expense@r1 :: Straight-line annual depreciation expense
  kind=definition expr=expense = (cost - residual) / life
  original: Annual depreciation = (cost - residual value) / useful life
  source_id=cfa-l1v3 locator=Learning Module 1, Issues in Expense Recognition, printed p22 (PDF p36), Example 3
  translation: The period count is dimensionless; output is the amount assigned to each full year.
  var cost         role=input   qty=depreciable_asset_cost                                  unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Cost assigned to the depreciable asset
  var residual     role=input   qty=asset_estimated_residual_value                          unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Estimated residual value
  var life         role=input   qty=asset_useful_life_years                                 unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Estimated useful life in annual accounting periods
  var expense      role=output  qty=straight_line_annual_depreciation                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Expense per full annual accounting period
  guards: ['cost >= residual', 'residual >= 0', 'life > 0']
  assumptions: ['Life is measured in full annual accounting periods.', 'Estimates remain unchanged over the represented allocation.']
  limitations: ['Partial periods, impairments and changes in estimates require separate treatment.']
  unresolved: []
  src [primary] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Issues in Expense Recognition, printed p22 (PDF p36), Example 3
      evidence=Straight-line depreciation allocates cost less residual value evenly across useful life.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] openstax-depreciation: Explain and Apply Depreciation Methods to Allocate Capitalized Costs
      url=https://openstax.org/books/principles-financial-accounting/pages/11-3-explain-and-apply-depreciation-methods-to-allocate-capitalized-costs accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Your Turn: straight-line and double-declining examples
      evidence=Straight-line allocation uses depreciable cost and life; declining-balance expense uses the beginning book value and accelerated rate.
      license=Terms may restrict reuse; original descriptions and formula-level facts only.
  ref cfa-zero-residual: {"cost": 10000, "residual": 0, "life": 5} -> 2000 (tol 1e-09)
      method=The CFA example allocates 10000 evenly over five years.
      provenance=CFA Example 3, printed p22/PDF p36.
  ref cfa-residual: {"cost": 10000, "residual": 4000, "life": 5} -> 1200 (tol 1e-09)
      method=The CFA example allocates the 6000 depreciable amount over five years.
      provenance=CFA Example 3, printed p22/PDF p36.
  rej [missing-input]: {"residual": 0, "life": 5} :: cost is required; no value is inferred from other inputs.
  inverse_notes: cost=expense*life+residual; residual=cost-expense*life; life=(cost-residual)/expense requires nonzero expense. If cost equals residual, life cannot be recovered from zero expense.
  integration: {"status": "requires-extension", "runtime_model": null, "gaps": ["Finance/statistics quantity meanings and their context contracts require registry review; research arithmetic is executable without a runtime projection."], "duplicate_of": []}

financial-analysis--declining-balance-depreciation-uncapped@r1 :: Declining-balance depreciation before residual-value cap
  kind=definition expr=expense = book * rate
  original: Depreciation = beginning net book value × declining-balance rate
  source_id=cfa-l1v3 locator=Learning Module 1, Issues in Expense Recognition, printed pp23–24 (PDF pp37–38), Example 4
  translation: Residual value is a separate external constraint, not subtracted in this rate calculation.
  var book         role=input   qty=asset_beginning_book_value                              unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Beginning-of-period depreciable asset carrying amount
  var rate         role=input   qty=declining_balance_depreciation_rate                     unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Rate for the full accounting period
  var expense      role=output  qty=uncapped_declining_balance_depreciation                 unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Calculated full-period expense before any residual-value floor
  guards: ['book >= 0', 'rate >= 0', 'rate <= 1']
  assumptions: ['This uncapped expression applies only when expense does not reduce carrying value below residual value.', 'Rate is the applicable full-period rate; partial periods require adjustment.']
  limitations: ['The final-period cap and method-switch decisions must be applied separately; this expression alone does not enforce the residual floor.']
  unresolved: []
  src [primary] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Issues in Expense Recognition, printed pp23–24 (PDF pp37–38), Example 4
      evidence=The example applies a declining-balance rate to beginning book value and stops depreciation at residual value.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] openstax-depreciation: Explain and Apply Depreciation Methods to Allocate Capitalized Costs
      url=https://openstax.org/books/principles-financial-accounting/pages/11-3-explain-and-apply-depreciation-methods-to-allocate-capitalized-costs accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Your Turn: straight-line and double-declining examples
      evidence=Straight-line allocation uses depreciable cost and life; declining-balance expense uses the beginning book value and accelerated rate.
      license=Terms may restrict reuse; original descriptions and formula-level facts only.
  ref cfa-year-one: {"book": 11000, "rate": 0.4} -> 4400 (tol 1e-09)
      method=Forty percent of 11000 is 4400.
      provenance=CFA Example 4, printed p23/PDF p37.
  ref cfa-year-two: {"book": 6600, "rate": 0.4} -> 2640 (tol 1e-09)
      method=Forty percent of 6600 is 2640.
      provenance=CFA Example 4, printed p23/PDF p37.
  rej [missing-input]: {"rate": 0.4} :: book is required; no value is inferred from other inputs.
  inverse_notes: book=expense/rate for rate>0; rate=expense/book for book>0. At zero book or rate the corresponding inverse is not unique.
  integration: {"status": "requires-extension", "runtime_model": null, "gaps": ["Finance/statistics quantity meanings and their context contracts require registry review; research arithmetic is executable without a runtime projection."], "duplicate_of": []}

financial-analysis--basic-earnings-per-common-share@r1 :: Basic earnings per common share
  kind=definition expr=eps = (income - preferred) / shares
  original: Basic EPS = (net income - preferred dividends) / weighted average common shares
  source_id=cfa-l1v3 locator=Learning Module 1, Earnings Per Share and Capital Structure and Basic EPS, printed p33 (PDF p47), Equation (1)
  translation: No rounding is applied; the currency unit records the amount per one common share.
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income_attributable_to_parent                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Period net income attributable to the parent before the indicated preferred deduction
  var preferred    role=input   qty=preferred_dividends_for_eps                             unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Preferred dividends or other required preferred allocation applicable to the period
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Positive number of shares
      def: Time-weighted common shares outstanding, adjusted for relevant splits
  var eps          role=output  qty=basic_earnings_per_common_share                         unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Basic earnings attributable to each weighted common share
  guards: ['shares > 0']
  assumptions: ['Numerator and shares cover the same reporting period and entity.', 'Preferred amount reflects applicable accounting treatment, including cumulative dividends when required.']
  limitations: ['Complex participating instruments and other adjustments require the appropriate reporting rules.', 'Shares are a dimensionless count and the result is currency per share, with the share basis explicit in context.']
  unresolved: []
  src [primary] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Earnings Per Share and Capital Structure and Basic EPS, printed p33 (PDF p47), Equation (1)
      evidence=Basic EPS allocates earnings after preferred dividends across time-weighted common shares.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] openstax-eps: Market Value Ratios
      url=https://openstax.org/books/principles-finance-2e/pages/6-5-market-value-ratios accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Calculating earnings per share, equation 6.27
      evidence=Basic EPS divides net income less preferred dividends by weighted average common shares.
      license=Terms may restrict reuse; original descriptions and formula-level facts only.
  ref cfa-example: {"income": 2500000, "preferred": 200000, "shares": 1125000} -> 2.0444444444444443 (tol 1e-09)
      method=2300000 divided by 1125000; CFA reports 2.04 after rounding.
      provenance=CFA Example 7, printed pp34–35/PDF pp48–49.
  ref loss: {"income": -100000, "preferred": 20000, "shares": 60000} -> -2 (tol 1e-09)
      method=A 120000 loss attributable to common shares over 60000 shares gives negative two.
      provenance=Curator-constructed independent numerical check; no empirical validation claimed.
  rej [missing-input]: {"preferred": 200000, "shares": 1125000} :: income is required; no value is inferred from other inputs.
  inverse_notes: income=eps*shares+preferred; shares=(income-preferred)/eps requires nonzero EPS. Zero earnings attributable to common imply zero EPS for any positive share count.
  integration: {"status": "requires-extension", "runtime_model": null, "gaps": ["Finance/statistics quantity meanings and their context contracts require registry review; research arithmetic is executable without a runtime projection."], "duplicate_of": []}

financial-analysis--if-converted-preferred-earnings-per-share@r1 :: If-converted EPS for convertible preferred shares
  kind=definition expr=eps = income / (shares + converted)
  original: Diluted EPS = net income / (weighted-average shares + shares from conversion)
  source_id=cfa-l1v3 locator=Learning Module 1, Diluted EPS: If-Converted Method, printed p36 (PDF p50), Equation (2) and Example 9
  translation: Share counts are dimensionless counts; the output is currency per share.
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income_attributable_to_parent                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Net income before preferred dividends; no other preferred classes
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Weighted-average existing common shares
  var converted    role=input   qty=weighted_average_preferred_conversion_shares            unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Time-weighted additional shares attributable to the assumed conversion
  var eps          role=output  qty=if_converted_preferred_eps                              unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Hypothetical earnings per common share after conversion
  guards: ['shares > 0', 'converted >= 0']
  assumptions: ['The convertible preferred is the only preferred class and shares are appropriately time-weighted.', 'Inclusion in reported diluted EPS requires a separate applicable antidilution assessment.']
  limitations: ['Other preferred classes and participating securities need additional numerator adjustments.']
  unresolved: []
  src [primary] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Diluted EPS: If-Converted Method, printed p36 (PDF p50), Equation (2) and Example 9
      evidence=For the single convertible preferred class example, conversion removes its preferred dividend and adds common shares.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] nyu-eps: Earnings Per Share class notes
      url=https://pages.stern.nyu.edu/~pzarowin/chapter13.htm accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Convertible securities and treasury stock paragraphs
      evidence=Conversion adds shares and reverses convertible preferred dividends or interest; options use assumed exercise and repurchase at average market price.
      license=Terms unknown; no redistribution of source text.
  ref cfa-preferred: {"income": 1750000, "shares": 500000, "converted": 100000} -> 2.9166666666666665 (tol 1e-09)
      method=1750000 divided by 600000; source rounds to 2.92.
      provenance=CFA Example 9, printed p36/PDF p50.
  ref second: {"income": 600000, "shares": 200000, "converted": 50000} -> 2.4 (tol 1e-09)
      method=600000 divided by 250000 shares.
      provenance=Curator-constructed independent numerical check; no empirical validation claimed.
  rej [missing-input]: {"shares": 500000, "converted": 100000} :: income is required; no value is inferred from other inputs.
  inverse_notes: income=eps*(shares+converted). Either share component can be recovered if the other is known and eps is nonzero; zero EPS makes the denominator unidentified.
  integration: {"status": "requires-extension", "runtime_model": null, "gaps": ["Finance/statistics quantity meanings and their context contracts require registry review; research arithmetic is executable without a runtime projection."], "duplicate_of": []}

financial-analysis--if-converted-debt-earnings-per-share@r1 :: If-converted EPS for convertible debt
  kind=definition expr=eps = (income + interest - preferred) / (shares + converted)
  original: Diluted EPS = (net income + after-tax convertible interest - preferred dividends) / (weighted shares + conversion shares)
  source_id=cfa-l1v3 locator=Learning Module 1, Diluted EPS: If-Converted Method, printed p37 (PDF p51), Equation (3) and Example 10
  translation: Shares are dimensionless counts; output is currency per share.
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income_attributable_to_parent                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Reported period net income
  var interest     role=input   qty=convertible_debt_after_tax_interest                     unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: After-tax interest saved by assumed conversion
  var preferred    role=input   qty=preferred_dividends_for_eps                             unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Preferred dividends applicable in the calculation
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Weighted-average existing common shares
  var converted    role=input   qty=weighted_average_debt_conversion_shares                 unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Time-weighted shares from assumed conversion
  var eps          role=output  qty=if_converted_debt_eps                                   unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Hypothetical EPS after debt conversion
  guards: ['shares > 0', 'converted >= 0']
  assumptions: ['After-tax interest and additional shares are matched to the period in which the debt was outstanding.', 'Inclusion in reported diluted EPS requires applicable antidilution testing.']
  limitations: ['Additional consequential income and tax effects or complex capital structures require separate adjustments.']
  unresolved: []
  src [primary] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Diluted EPS: If-Converted Method, printed p37 (PDF p51), Equation (3) and Example 10
      evidence=Debt conversion adds after-tax interest to earnings and assumed shares to the denominator.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] kpmg-eps: Earnings per share handbook
      url=https://kpmg.com/kpmg-us/content/dam/kpmg/frv/pdf/2023/handbook-earnings-per-share.pdf accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=PDF p195, convertible bond example footnote; p344, dilutive assessment
      evidence=Convertible bond interest is added back net of tax and conversion shares enter the denominator, subject to dilution testing.
      license=Copyright KPMG; equation-level factual paraphrase only.
  ref cfa-bond: {"income": 750000, "interest": 2100, "preferred": 0, "shares": 690000, "converted": 10000} -> 1.0744285714285715 (tol 1e-09)
      method=752100 divided by 700000; source rounds to 1.07.
      provenance=CFA Example 10 and Exhibit 12, printed pp37–38/PDF pp51–52.
  ref second: {"income": 500000, "interest": 10000, "preferred": 10000, "shares": 200000, "converted": 50000} -> 2 (tol 1e-09)
      method=500000 adjusted earnings divided by 250000 shares.
      provenance=Curator-constructed independent numerical check; no empirical validation claimed.
  rej [missing-input]: {"interest": 2100, "preferred": 0, "shares": 690000, "converted": 10000} :: income is required; no value is inferred from other inputs.
  inverse_notes: income=eps*(shares+converted)-interest+preferred. A share-count inverse requires nonzero EPS; denominator remains positive.
  integration: {"status": "requires-extension", "runtime_model": null, "gaps": ["Finance/statistics quantity meanings and their context contracts require registry review; research arithmetic is executable without a runtime projection."], "duplicate_of": []}

financial-analysis--treasury-stock-method-earnings-per-share@r1 :: Treasury-stock method EPS for options
  kind=definition expr=eps = (income - preferred) / (shares + (issued - repurchased) * fraction)
  original: Diluted EPS = (net income - preferred dividends) / [weighted shares + (option shares - shares repurchased) × period fraction]
  source_id=cfa-l1v3 locator=Learning Module 1, Diluted EPS: Treasury Stock Method, printed p39 (PDF p53), Equation (4) and Example 11
  translation: Counts are dimensionless and the EPS output is currency per common share. Repurchased shares are computed upstream from proceeds and the average market price.
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income_attributable_to_parent                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Period net income
  var preferred    role=input   qty=preferred_dividends_for_eps                             unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Preferred dividends deducted for common earnings
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Weighted-average common shares
  var issued       role=input   qty=shares_assumed_issued_on_option_exercise                unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Shares assumed issued upon exercise
  var repurchased  role=input   qty=shares_assumed_repurchased_with_option_proceeds         unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Shares hypothetically repurchased with exercise proceeds at period-average market price
  var fraction     role=input   qty=option_outstanding_period_fraction                      unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Fraction of reporting period in which the options were outstanding
  var eps          role=output  qty=treasury_stock_method_eps                               unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Hypothetical EPS for the selected option class
  guards: ['shares > 0', 'issued >= repurchased', 'repurchased >= 0', 'fraction >= 0', 'fraction <= 1']
  assumptions: ['The stated option class is dilutive under the applicable control-number test.', 'Issued and repurchased counts use the same exercise assumptions and are weighted once.']
  limitations: ['Do not include antidilutive options in reported diluted EPS; other instruments and special proceeds require separate treatment.']
  unresolved: []
  src [primary] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Diluted EPS: Treasury Stock Method, printed p39 (PDF p53), Equation (4) and Example 11
      evidence=The option exercise increases shares by the time-weighted difference between issued and repurchased shares.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] nyu-eps: Earnings Per Share class notes
      url=https://pages.stern.nyu.edu/~pzarowin/chapter13.htm accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Convertible securities and treasury stock paragraphs
      evidence=Conversion adds shares and reverses convertible preferred dividends or interest; options use assumed exercise and repurchase at average market price.
      license=Terms unknown; no redistribution of source text.
  ref cfa-options: {"income": 2300000, "preferred": 0, "shares": 800000, "issued": 30000, "repurchased": 19090.90909090909, "fraction": 1} -> 2.836322869955157 (tol 1e-08)
      method=2300000 divided by approximately 810909.09; CFA rounds to 2.84.
      provenance=CFA Example 11, printed pp39–40/PDF pp53–54.
  ref half-year: {"income": 1000000, "preferred": 0, "shares": 400000, "issued": 40000, "repurchased": 20000, "fraction": 0.5} -> 2.4390243902439024 (tol 1e-09)
      method=A 10000 time-weighted share increase gives 1000000/410000.
      provenance=Curator-constructed independent numerical check; no empirical validation claimed.
  rej [missing-input]: {"preferred": 0, "shares": 800000, "issued": 30000, "repurchased": 19090.90909090909, "fraction": 1} :: income is required; no value is inferred from other inputs.
  inverse_notes: income=eps*(shares+(issued-repurchased)*fraction)+preferred; share inversions require nonzero EPS and fraction.
  integration: {"status": "requires-extension", "runtime_model": null, "gaps": ["Finance/statistics quantity meanings and their context contracts require registry review; research arithmetic is executable without a runtime projection."], "duplicate_of": []}

financial-analysis--income-statement-common-size-fraction@r1 :: Income-statement common-size fraction
  kind=definition expr=ratio = amount / revenue
  original: Income-statement common-size fraction = income-statement line item / revenue
  source_id=cfa-l1v3 locator=Learning Module 1, Common-Size Analysis, printed p43 (PDF p57), Exhibit 15
  translation: Currency cancels and the output is a base-one fraction. The gross, operating, pretax and net margins are meaningful named selections of the comparison item; each is also described separately.
  var amount       role=input   qty=income_statement_line_item                              unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Signed income-statement line item for the chosen entity and reporting period
  var revenue      role=input   qty=income_statement_revenue                                unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Positive revenue for the same entity and reporting period
  var ratio        role=output  qty=income_statement_common_size_fraction                   unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: income-statement line item as a fraction of revenue
  guards: ['revenue > 0']
  assumptions: ['The numerator and revenue use the same currency, entity, accounting basis and period.', 'Revenue is positive in this analytical profile.']
  limitations: ['Changes in classification or reporting scope can affect comparisons across statements.', 'Negative numerator values are permitted; percent display is not part of the calculation.']
  unresolved: []
  src [primary] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Common-Size Analysis, printed p43 (PDF p57), Exhibit 15
      evidence=Income-statement common-size fraction expresses income-statement line item relative to revenue.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] openstax-vertical: Financial Statement Analysis
      url=https://openstax.org/books/principles-financial-accounting/pages/a-financial-statement-analysis accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Vertical Analysis; common-size percentage
      evidence=An income statement uses net sales as the base and divides each comparison item by the base.
      license=OpenStax terms include restrictions; formula-level facts only.
  ref sales-cost: {"amount": 3000000, "revenue": 10000000} -> 0.3 (tol 1e-09)
      method=Company A cost of sales is 30% of sales.
      provenance=CFA Exhibit 15, printed p43/PDF p57.
  ref operating-profit: {"amount": 400000, "revenue": 2000000} -> 0.2 (tol 1e-09)
      method=Company C operating profit is 20% of sales.
      provenance=CFA Exhibit 15, printed p43/PDF p57.
  rej [missing-input]: {"revenue": 10000000} :: amount is required; no value is inferred from other inputs.
  inverse_notes: amount=ratio*revenue. revenue=amount/ratio requires a nonzero ratio; if amount and ratio are both zero, revenue is unidentified.
  integration: {"status": "requires-extension", "runtime_model": null, "gaps": ["Finance/statistics quantity meanings and their context contracts require registry review; research arithmetic is executable without a runtime projection."], "duplicate_of": []}

financial-analysis--gross-profit-margin@r1 :: Gross profit margin
  kind=definition expr=ratio = amount / revenue
  original: Gross profit margin = gross profit / revenue
  source_id=cfa-l1v3 locator=Learning Module 1, Income Statement Ratios, printed pp45–46 (PDF pp59–60), Exhibit 17
  translation: Currency cancels and the output is a base-one fraction. This is a named common-size fraction; gross profit itself is already covered by financial-analysis--gross-profit.

financial-analysis--straight-line-depreciation-expense@r2 :: Straight-line annual depreciation expense
  kind=definition expr=expense = (cost - residual) / life
  original: Annual depreciation = (cost - residual value) / useful life
  source_id=openstax-depreciation locator=Your Turn: straight-line and double-declining examples
  translation: The period count is dimensionless; output is the amount assigned to each full year. The owner PDF locator is retained on the project-derivation source; relationship locator identifies the accessible corroborating source.
  var cost         role=input   qty=depreciable_asset_cost                                  unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Cost assigned to the depreciable asset
  var residual     role=input   qty=asset_estimated_residual_value                          unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Estimated residual value
  var life         role=input   qty=asset_useful_life_years                                 unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Estimated useful life in annual accounting periods
  var expense      role=output  qty=straight_line_annual_depreciation                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Expense per full annual accounting period
  guards: ['cost >= residual', 'residual >= 0', 'life > 0']
  assumptions: ['Life is measured in full annual accounting periods.', 'Estimates remain unchanged over the represented allocation.']
  limitations: ['Partial periods, impairments and changes in estimates require separate treatment.']
  unresolved: []
  src [project-derivation] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Issues in Expense Recognition, printed p22 (PDF p36), Example 3
      evidence=Straight-line depreciation allocates cost less residual value evenly across useful life.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] openstax-depreciation: Explain and Apply Depreciation Methods to Allocate Capitalized Costs
      url=https://openstax.org/books/principles-financial-accounting/pages/11-3-explain-and-apply-depreciation-methods-to-allocate-capitalized-costs accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Your Turn: straight-line and double-declining examples
      evidence=Straight-line allocation uses depreciable cost and life; declining-balance expense uses the beginning book value and accelerated rate.
      license=Terms may restrict reuse; original descriptions and formula-level facts only.
  ref cfa-zero-residual: {"cost": 10000, "residual": 0, "life": 5} -> 2000 (tol 1e-09)
      method=The CFA example allocates 10000 evenly over five years.
      provenance=CFA Example 3, printed p22/PDF p36.
  ref cfa-residual: {"cost": 10000, "residual": 4000, "life": 5} -> 1200 (tol 1e-09)
      method=The CFA example allocates the 6000 depreciable amount over five years.
      provenance=CFA Example 3, printed p22/PDF p36.
  rej [missing-input]: {"residual": 0, "life": 5} :: cost is required; no value is inferred from other inputs.
  inverse_notes: cost=expense*life+residual; residual=cost-expense*life; life=(cost-residual)/expense requires nonzero expense. If cost equals residual, life cannot be recovered from zero expense.

financial-analysis--basic-earnings-per-common-share@r2 :: Basic earnings per common share
  kind=definition expr=eps = (income - preferred) / shares
  original: Basic EPS = (net income - preferred dividends) / weighted average common shares
  source_id=openstax-eps locator=Calculating earnings per share, equation 6.27
  translation: No rounding is applied; the currency unit records the amount per one common share. The owner PDF locator is retained on the project-derivation source; relationship locator identifies the accessible corroborating source.
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income_attributable_to_parent                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Period net income attributable to the parent before the indicated preferred deduction
  var preferred    role=input   qty=preferred_dividends_for_eps                             unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Preferred dividends or other required preferred allocation applicable to the period
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Positive number of shares
      def: Time-weighted common shares outstanding, adjusted for relevant splits
  var eps          role=output  qty=basic_earnings_per_common_share                         unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Basic earnings attributable to each weighted common share
  guards: ['shares > 0']
  assumptions: ['Numerator and shares cover the same reporting period and entity.', 'Preferred amount reflects applicable accounting treatment, including cumulative dividends when required.']
  limitations: ['Complex participating instruments and other adjustments require the appropriate reporting rules.', 'Shares are a dimensionless count and the result is currency per share, with the share basis explicit in context.']

financial-analysis--if-converted-preferred-earnings-per-share@r2 :: If-converted EPS for convertible preferred shares
  kind=definition expr=eps = income / (shares + converted)
  original: Diluted EPS = net income / (weighted-average shares + shares from conversion)
  source_id=nyu-eps locator=Convertible securities and treasury stock paragraphs
  translation: Share counts are dimensionless counts; the output is currency per share. The owner PDF locator is retained on the project-derivation source; relationship locator identifies the accessible corroborating source.
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income_attributable_to_parent                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Net income before preferred dividends; no other preferred classes
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Weighted-average existing common shares
  var converted    role=input   qty=weighted_average_preferred_conversion_shares            unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Time-weighted additional shares attributable to the assumed conversion
  var eps          role=output  qty=if_converted_preferred_eps                              unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Hypothetical earnings per common share after conversion
  guards: ['shares > 0', 'converted >= 0']
  assumptions: ['The convertible preferred is the only preferred class and shares are appropriately time-weighted.', 'Inclusion in reported diluted EPS requires a separate applicable antidilution assessment.']
  limitations: ['Other preferred classes and participating securities need additional numerator adjustments.']
  unresolved: []
  src [project-derivation] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Diluted EPS: If-Converted Method, printed p36 (PDF p50), Equation (2) and Example 9
      evidence=For the single convertible preferred class example, conversion removes its preferred dividend and adds common shares.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution

financial-analysis--if-converted-debt-earnings-per-share@r2 :: If-converted EPS for convertible debt
  kind=definition expr=eps = (income + interest - preferred) / (shares + converted)
  original: Diluted EPS = (net income + after-tax convertible interest - preferred dividends) / (weighted shares + conversion shares)
  source_id=kpmg-eps locator=PDF p195, convertible bond example footnote; p344, dilutive assessment
  translation: Shares are dimensionless counts; output is currency per share. The owner PDF locator is retained on the project-derivation source; relationship locator identifies the accessible corroborating source.

  var preferred    role=input   qty=preferred_dividends_for_eps                             unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Preferred dividends applicable in the calculation
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Weighted-average existing common shares
  var converted    role=input   qty=weighted_average_debt_conversion_shares                 unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Time-weighted shares from assumed conversion

financial-analysis--treasury-stock-method-earnings-per-share@r2 :: Treasury-stock method EPS for options
  kind=definition expr=eps = (income - preferred) / (shares + (issued - repurchased) * fraction)
  original: Diluted EPS = (net income - preferred dividends) / [weighted shares + (option shares - shares repurchased) × period fraction]
  source_id=nyu-eps locator=Convertible securities and treasury stock paragraphs
  translation: Counts are dimensionless and the EPS output is currency per common share. Repurchased shares are computed upstream from proceeds and the average market price. The owner PDF locator is retained on the project-derivation source; relationship locator identifies the accessible corroborating source.
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income_attributable_to_parent                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Period net income
  var preferred    role=input   qty=preferred_dividends_for_eps                             unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Preferred dividends deducted for common earnings
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Weighted-average common shares
  var issued       role=input   qty=shares_assumed_issued_on_option_exercise                unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Shares assumed issued upon exercise
  var repurchased  role=input   qty=shares_assumed_repurchased_with_option_proceeds         unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Nonnegative share count unless otherwise guarded
      def: Shares hypothetically repurchased with exercise proceeds at period-average market price
  var fraction     role=input   qty=option_outstanding_period_fraction                      unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Fraction of reporting period in which the options were outstanding
  var eps          role=output  qty=treasury_stock_method_eps                               unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Hypothetical EPS for the selected option class
  guards: ['shares > 0', 'issued >= repurchased', 'repurchased >= 0', 'fraction >= 0', 'fraction <= 1']
  assumptions: ['The stated option class is dilutive under the applicable control-number test.', 'Issued and repurchased counts use the same exercise assumptions and are weighted once.']
  limitations: ['Do not include antidilutive options in reported diluted EPS; other instruments and special proceeds require separate treatment.']
  unresolved: []

      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Learning Module 1, Diluted EPS: Treasury Stock Method, printed p39 (PDF p53), Equation (4) and Example 11

  original: Income-statement common-size fraction = income-statement line item / revenue

      def: Positive revenue for the same entity and reporting period

      def: income-statement line item as a fraction of revenue
  guards: ['revenue > 0']

financial-analysis--balance-sheet-common-size-fraction@r1 :: Balance-sheet common-size fraction
  kind=definition expr=ratio = item / assets
  original: Common-size balance-sheet item = balance-sheet item / total assets

  original: Cash collected = revenue - increase in accounts receivable
  source_id=openstax-direct-cash locator=Cash collected from customers; cash paid to suppliers; cash paid for salaries

      def: ending less beginning accounts receivable

      def: cash collected from customers

  kind=definition expr=ratio = cfo / base
  original: Operating cash-flow debt coverage = operating cash flow / total debt
  source_id=cfa-cash-coverage locator=Printed p14, Figures 9–10: performance and coverage ratios
  translation: Currency cancels; debt is a balance while payments are flows and must be bound according to their definitions.
  var cfo          role=input   qty=cash_flow_from_operating_activities                     unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: cash flow from operations

      def: total debt
  var ratio        role=output  qty=operating_cash_flow_debt_coverage                       unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Operating cash flow divided by total debt
  guards: ['base > 0']
  assumptions: ['CFO and the denominator refer to the same entity and consistent financial period; the debt balance is measured at the chosen reporting date.', 'The denominator is a positive amount on the declared accounting basis.']
  limitations: ['Classification differences and nonrecurring items affect comparisons; negative CFO is permitted.']

financial-analysis--operating-cash-flow-per-common-share@r1 :: Operating cash flow per common share
  kind=definition expr=result = (cfo - preferred) / shares
  original: Cash flow per share = (CFO - preferred dividends) / number of common shares outstanding
  source_id=cfa-cash-coverage locator=Printed p14, Figures 9–10: performance and coverage ratios
  translation: Shares are dimensionless counts; output is currency per share. Prevent double-counting preferred dividends when operating cash flow already includes them.
  var cfo          role=input   qty=cash_flow_from_operating_activities                     unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: operating cash flow
  var preferred    role=input   qty=preferred_dividends_paid                                unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: preferred dividends
  var shares       role=input   qty=common_shares_outstanding                               unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Positive common-share count, with date or averaging convention specified
  var result       role=output  qty=operating_cash_flow_per_common_share                    unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Cash flow after preferred dividends per common share
  guards: ['shares > 0']
  assumptions: ['CFO is adjusted for any total dividends already included as operating outflow before subtracting preferred dividends.', 'Share count convention, entity, period and currency are explicit and compatible.']
  limitations: ['The source formula names shares outstanding; weighted-average and ending-share variants are not interchangeable without a declared convention.', 'Other distributions and participating equity require separate treatment.']
  unresolved: []
  src [project-derivation] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers

financial-analysis--indirect-operating-cash-flow-reconciliation@r1 :: Indirect reconciliation of operating cash flow
  kind=definition expr=cfo = income + noncash - gain - assets + liabilities
  original: CFO = net income + noncash adjustments - nonoperating gains - increase in operating assets + increase in operating liabilities
  source_id=openstax-indirect locator=Steps 1 and 4; Prepare the Operating Activities Section
  translation: Operating asset and liability changes are ending minus beginning; negative net nonoperating gains represent losses.
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income                                              unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: net income
  var noncash      role=input   qty=net_noncash_income_adjustment                           unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: net noncash adjustments
  var gain         role=input   qty=net_nonoperating_gains_in_income                        unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: net nonoperating gains
  var assets       role=input   qty=operating_current_assets_change                         unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: increase in operating current assets
  var liabilities  role=input   qty=operating_current_liabilities_change                    unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: increase in operating current liabilities
  var cfo          role=output  qty=cash_flow_from_operating_activities                     unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: cash flow from operations
  guards: []
  assumptions: ['Noncash, nonoperating and operating balance-change aggregates are complete, correctly signed and nonoverlapping.', 'Balance changes exclude acquisition, disposal, currency translation and other movements not reconciling operating cash flow.']
  limitations: ['Aggregated inputs are prepared externally and cannot be automatically checked for omissions or double-counting.', 'Accounting classification of interest, dividends and taxes can change the relevant adjustments.']
  unresolved: []
  src [project-derivation] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Financial Statement Analysis Learning Module 3, Overall Statement of Cash Flows Under the Indirect Method, printed pp149–151 (PDF pp163–165), Exhibits 9–10
      evidence=Net income is reconciled to operating cash flow using noncash, nonoperating and working-capital adjustments.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] openstax-indirect: Prepare the Statement of Cash Flows Using the Indirect Method
      url=https://openstax.org/books/principles-financial-accounting/pages/16-3-prepare-the-statement-of-cash-flows-using-the-indirect-method accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Steps 1 and 4; Prepare the Operating Activities Section
      evidence=The indirect method adjusts net income for noncash expenses, investing gains or losses, and current operating asset and liability movements.
      license=OpenStax page states noncommercial and AI-use restrictions; no source pages redistributed.
  ref openstax-example: {"income": 4340, "noncash": 14400, "gain": 4800, "assets": -1300, "liabilities": -1400} -> 13840 (tol 1e-09)
      method=Aggregate asset change is -4500+700+2500=-1300; liability change is -1800+400=-1400.
      provenance=OpenStax section 16.3 operating example.

financial-analysis--retained-earnings-roll-forward@r1 :: Retained earnings roll-forward without other adjustments
  kind=definition expr=ending = beginning + income - dividends
  original: Ending retained earnings = beginning retained earnings + net income - dividends
  source_id=openstax-retained locator=Statement of Retained Earnings
  translation: Net income and retained earnings may be negative; dividends are the amount charged, not inferred cash payments.
  var beginning    role=input   qty=beginning_retained_earnings                             unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: beginning retained earnings
  var income       role=input   qty=net_income                                              unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: net income
  var dividends    role=input   qty=dividends_charged_to_retained_earnings                  unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: dividends charged to retained earnings
  var ending       role=output  qty=ending_retained_earnings                                unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: ending retained earnings
  guards: []
  assumptions: ['No prior-period corrections, direct equity adjustments, stock dividends or other retained-earnings entries occur.', 'All amounts apply to the same entity and reporting period.']
  limitations: ['Dividends charged to retained earnings need not equal cash dividends paid if dividend payables or noncash dividends change.']

financial-analysis--inventory-turnover-ratio@r1 :: Inventory turnover
  kind=definition expr=turnover = flow / balance
  original: Inventory turnover = cost of goods sold / average inventory
  source_id=cfa-ratio-list locator=p1, ratios 5, 7, 9 and 14
  translation: Amounts share one currency; the quotient counts turnovers over the declared financial period. Different denominators define different economic measures.
  var flow         role=input   qty=cost_of_goods_sold                                      unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: cost of goods sold for the stated period and accounting scope
  var balance      role=input   qty=average_inventory                                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: average inventory for the same period and entity
  var turnover     role=output  qty=inventory_turnover_ratio                                unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Amount of cost of goods sold per unit of average inventory in the stated period
  guards: ['flow >= 0', 'balance > 0']
  assumptions: ['The flow and average balance use the same entity, currency, accounting basis and period.', 'The chosen averaging convention is specified and applied consistently.']
  limitations: ['Inventory write-downs and seasonality affect the cost and average-balance measures.', 'A quarterly turnover is per quarter and must not be compared with an annual turnover without an explicit period adjustment.']
  unresolved: []
  src [project-derivation] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03

  src [primary] cfa-ratio-list: Financial Ratio List
      url=https://www.cfainstitute.org/sites/default/files/-/media/documents/support/programs/cfa/cfa_program_level_ii_financial_ratio_list.pdf accessed=2026-11-03

      method=The CFA example divides 120000 cost of sales by 10000 average inventory.

financial-analysis--days-of-inventory-on-hand@r1 :: Days of inventory on hand
  kind=definition expr=days = period / turnover
  original: Days of inventory on hand = period length / matching turnover
  source_id=cfa-ratio-list locator=p1, ratios 6, 8, 10 and 11
  translation: Period is a physical duration in days, not an annualization factor; output retains the same duration unit. Turnover is a dimensionless count over that period.
  var period       role=input   qty=reporting_period_duration                               unit=d        dims=time          domain=Positive duration
      def: Physical length of the reporting period
  var turnover     role=input   qty=inventory_turnover_ratio                                unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Positive turnover count for that same period
  var days         role=output  qty=days_of_inventory_on_hand                               unit=d        dims=time          domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Days of inventory on hand as an elapsed duration
  guards: ['period > 0', 'turnover > 0']
  assumptions: ['Turnover and period duration cover the same entity, accounting period and averaging convention.', 'A year can contain 365, 366 or another number of days; it is supplied rather than hard-coded.']
  limitations: ['Turnover is an accounting approximation and does not identify the actual holding time of each individual item.', 'Zero turnover has no finite duration in this model.']
  unresolved: []
  src [project-derivation] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers

      url=https://www.cfainstitute.org/sites/default/files/-/media/documents/support/programs/cfa/cfa_program_level_ii_financial_ratio_list.pdf accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=p1, ratios 6, 8, 10 and 11
      evidence=The public list defines receivables, inventory and payable days as period duration divided by the corresponding turnover and defines the cash conversion cycle.
      license=CFA Institute rights reserved; individual formulas and original paraphrases only.

financial-analysis--cash-conversion-cycle@r1 :: Cash conversion cycle
  kind=definition expr=cycle = inventory + receivables - payables
  original: Cash conversion cycle = days inventory on hand + days sales outstanding - days of payables
  source_id=cfa-ratio-list locator=p1, ratios 6, 8, 10 and 11
  translation: All quantities are physical durations in days. A negative result is meaningful and is not excluded.
  var inventory    role=input   qty=days_of_inventory_on_hand                               unit=d        dims=time          domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Inventory duration
  var receivables  role=input   qty=days_of_sales_outstanding                               unit=d        dims=time          domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Receivables duration
  var payables     role=input   qty=days_of_trade_payables                                  unit=d        dims=time          domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Payables duration
  var cycle        role=output  qty=cash_conversion_cycle                                   unit=d        dims=time          domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Signed net operating cash conversion duration
  guards: ['inventory >= 0', 'receivables >= 0', 'payables >= 0']
  assumptions: ['All durations use the same period and underlying operating scope.']
  limitations: ['The cycle may be negative when payment to suppliers follows customer collection; the expression is an approximation based on aggregate ratios.']
  unresolved: []

financial-analysis--working-capital-turnover-ratio@r1 :: Working capital turnover
  kind=definition expr=turnover = revenue / base
  original: Working capital turnover = revenue / average working capital
  source_id=cfa-ratio-list locator=p1, ratios 4, 12 and 13
  translation: Currency cancels. The output is a count of turnovers within the declared financial period.
  var revenue      role=input   qty=net_period_revenue                                      unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: total revenue
  var base         role=input   qty=average_working_capital                                 unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: average working capital
  var turnover     role=output  qty=working_capital_turnover_ratio                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Revenue per unit of average working capital over the stated period
  guards: ['revenue >= 0', 'base > 0']
  assumptions: ['Revenue and average balance cover the same entity, currency and period.', 'The positive average-balance analytical profile and averaging method are explicit.']
  limitations: ['A negative or zero average denominator is outside this profile; compare periods only after aligning their lengths.', 'Classification and accounting methods may differ across entities.']

financial-analysis--defensive-interval-from-period-expenditures@r1 :: Defensive interval from period cash expenditures
  kind=definition expr=interval = (cash + securities + receivables) * period / expenses
  original: Defensive interval = (cash + short-term marketable investments + receivables) / daily cash expenditures
  source_id=cfa-ratio-list locator=p1, ratios 4, 12 and 13
  translation: This is the source ratio with daily expenditures replaced by period cash expenditures divided by physical period duration; output uses days.
  var cash         role=input   qty=cash_and_cash_equivalents                               unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: cash
  var securities   role=input   qty=short_term_marketable_securities                        unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: short-term marketable investments
  var receivables  role=input   qty=current_trade_receivables                               unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: receivables
  var period       role=input   qty=reporting_period_duration                               unit=d        dims=time          domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Physical duration of the expenditure measurement period
  var expenses     role=input   qty=period_cash_operating_expenditures                      unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: period cash expenditures
  var interval     role=output  qty=defensive_interval_duration                             unit=d        dims=time          domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Elapsed duration for which specified liquid assets cover daily cash spending
  guards: ['cash >= 0', 'securities >= 0', 'receivables >= 0', 'period > 0', 'expenses > 0']
  assumptions: ['The denominator measures recurring cash expenditure and excludes noncash expense; the period matches the expenditure.', 'The estimate assumes the specified daily rate is maintained and no additional cash inflow.']
  limitations: ['Taxes and irregular expenditures require explicit treatment; the result is a simplifying liquidity measure rather than a cash forecast.']
  unresolved: []

  assumptions: ['Inputs cover the same reporting entity, currency and period.', 'Income subtotals follow consistent accounting definitions.']

financial-analysis--pretax-return-on-total-capital@r1 :: Pretax return on total capital
  kind=definition expr=ratio = ebit / (debt + equity)
  original: Return on total capital = EBIT / average short- and long-term debt and equity
  source_id=cfa-ratio-list locator=p1, ratios 22, 25, 26 and 27
  translation: This return uses pretax EBIT and an average invested capital balance, not net income or total assets.
  var ebit         role=input   qty=earnings_before_interest_and_taxes                      unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: earnings before interest and taxes
  var debt         role=input   qty=average_interest_bearing_debt                           unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: average interest-bearing debt
  var equity       role=input   qty=average_shareholders_equity                             unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: average shareholders equity
  var ratio        role=output  qty=pretax_return_on_total_capital                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: EBIT relative to average debt and equity capital
  guards: ['debt >= 0', 'debt + equity > 0']
  assumptions: ['Debt and equity averages use consistent dates, entity, currency and period.', 'Capital means interest-bearing debt plus shareholders equity in this definition.']
  limitations: ['Other invested-capital definitions can treat cash, leases and operating liabilities differently.', 'The denominator must be positive under this analytical profile.']
  unresolved: []

financial-analysis--dupont-three-factor-return-on-equity@r1 :: Three-factor DuPont return on equity
  kind=definition expr=roe = margin * turnover * leverage
  original: ROE = net profit margin × total asset turnover × financial leverage
  source_id=cfa-revenue-recognition locator=Printed p13, Figure 8: DuPont ROE formula
  translation: All factors are dimensionless. This is a recognized decomposition of the independently defined return on equity and depends on the individual ratio records.
  var margin       role=input   qty=net_profit_margin                                       unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Net income divided by matching revenue
  var turnover     role=input   qty=total_asset_turnover_ratio                              unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Revenue divided by matching average total assets
  var leverage     role=input   qty=financial_leverage_ratio                                unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Matching average assets divided by average equity
  var roe          role=output  qty=return_on_average_equity                                unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Net income divided by average equity
  guards: []
  assumptions: ['Factors must be computed from the same entity, period, income and averaging conventions.', 'Revenue, average assets and average equity used to form the factors must be nonzero.']
  limitations: ['The equation is an algebraic decomposition, not a causal estimate.', 'Precomputed factors must be compatible upstream; this scalar expression cannot enforce common underlying balances.']
  unresolved: []
  src [project-derivation] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Financial Statement Analysis Learning Module 4, DuPont Analysis, printed p225 (PDF p239), Equation (2)
      evidence=Return on equity decomposes into net margin, asset turnover and leverage.
      license=Purchased owner copy; personal-use terms assumed; no redistribution
  src [primary] cfa-revenue-recognition: Revenue Recognition: Top Ten Questions Investors Should Ask
      url=https://rpc.cfainstitute.org/sites/default/files/-/media/documents/article/position-paper/revenue-recognition-top-ten-questions-investors-should-ask.pdf accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Printed p13, Figure 8: DuPont ROE formula
      evidence=Figure 8 shows the return-on-equity decomposition through profitability, turnover, leverage, tax and interest burden.
      license=Copyright CFA Institute; formulas and original paraphrases only.
  ref positive: {"margin": 0.1, "turnover": 2, "leverage": 1.5} -> 0.3 (tol 1e-09)
      method=10% margin times two turnovers times 1.5 leverage gives 30%.
      provenance=Curator-constructed independent numerical check; no empirical validation claimed.
  ref loss: {"margin": -0.05, "turnover": 2, "leverage": 2} -> -0.2 (tol 1e-09)

financial-analysis--dupont-five-factor-return-on-equity@r1 :: Five-factor DuPont return on equity
  kind=definition expr=roe = tax * interest * margin * turnover * leverage
  original: ROE = tax burden × interest burden × EBIT margin × asset turnover × leverage
  source_id=cfa-revenue-recognition locator=Printed p13, Figure 8: DuPont ROE formula
  translation: The five-factor representation expands the net margin in the three-factor identity; both are reported because the source defines and uses their different component decompositions.
  var tax          role=input   qty=dupont_tax_burden                                       unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Net income divided by pretax income
  var interest     role=input   qty=dupont_interest_burden                                  unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Pretax income divided by EBIT
  var margin       role=input   qty=dupont_ebit_margin                                      unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: EBIT divided by revenue
  var turnover     role=input   qty=total_asset_turnover_ratio                              unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Revenue divided by average total assets
  var leverage     role=input   qty=financial_leverage_ratio                                unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Average assets divided by average equity
  var roe          role=output  qty=return_on_average_equity                                unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Net income divided by average equity
  guards: []
  assumptions: ['All factors use matching accounting scopes and periods; EBIT and pretax income are nonzero when constructing their ratios.', 'Average assets and equity and revenue are nonzero where used as denominators.']
  limitations: ['The decomposition does not establish causation; intermediate zero subtotals can make individual factors undefined.', 'Consistent source quantities must be checked externally before combining factors.']
  unresolved: []
  src [project-derivation] cfa-l1v3: CFA Program Curriculum 2023 Level I Volume 3: Financial Statement Analysis, Corporate Issuers
      url=None accessed=2026-11-03
      locator=Financial Statement Analysis Learning Module 4, DuPont Analysis, printed p227 (PDF p241), Equation (3)

      locator=Printed p13, Figure 8: DuPont ROE formula
      evidence=Figure 8 shows the return-on-equity decomposition through profitability, turnover, leverage, tax and interest burden.
      license=Copyright CFA Institute; formulas and original paraphrases only.
  ref positive: {"tax": 0.8, "interest": 0.9, "margin": 0.1, "turnover": 2, "leverage": 1.5} -> 0.216 (tol 1e-09)
      method=0.8×0.9×0.1×2×1.5 = 0.216.
      provenance=Curator-constructed independent numerical check; no empirical validation claimed.

      def: FIFO net income

financial-analysis--lifo-reserve-definition@r2 :: LIFO inventory reserve
  kind=definition expr=reserve = fifo - lifo
  original: LIFO reserve = FIFO inventory - LIFO inventory
  source_id=wiley-inventory-excerpt locator=Reading 16, The LIFO Method, LIFO Reserve; practice solution 3 for after-tax income conversion
  translation: The FIFO adjustment fifo=lifo+reserve is the inverse of this definition, not a separate relationship.
  var fifo         role=input   qty=fifo_inventory_carrying_amount                          unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar

  var lifo         role=input   qty=lifo_inventory_carrying_amount                          unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: LIFO inventory
  var reserve      role=output  qty=lifo_inventory_reserve                                  unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: LIFO reserve
  guards: []
  assumptions: ['Inventory amounts refer to the same entity, date, scope and currency.']
  limitations: ['The reserve need not always be positive; definition and disclosure conventions must be checked.']

      def: interest payments

      def: Count of identical units released from the specified older layer
  var current      role=input   qty=inventory_current_cost_per_unit                         unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: current replacement cost per unit
  var historical   role=input   qty=inventory_layer_cost_per_unit                           unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: historical cost per unit
  var effect       role=output  qty=lifo_liquidation_gross_profit_effect                    unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: gross profit effect
  guards: ['units >= 0', 'current >= 0', 'historical >= 0']
  assumptions: ['All released units are from a homogeneous cost layer and share the stated replacement cost.', 'The comparison holds sales volume and price constant and isolates the accounting cost difference.']
  limitations: ['The result is a relative pretax gross-profit effect, not a cash flow or proof of operational improvement.', 'Multiple layers or replacement costs require separate effects to be summed.']
  unresolved: []

  src [primary] understanding-accounting-lifo-liquidation: LIFO liquidation
      url=https://understandingaccounting.org/concepts/lifo-liquidation/ accessed=2026-11-03

      evidence=The bounded accounting effect multiplies released older units by the difference between current and historical unit costs.
      license=Exact terms unknown; no broader reuse permission asserted.
  ref cfa-example: {"units": 1000, "current": 115, "historical": 100} -> 15000 (tol 1e-09)
      method=1000 units times the 15 per-unit cost difference gives 15000.

financial-analysis--cash-interest-coverage-ratio@r1 :: Cash interest coverage ratio
  kind=definition expr=ratio = (cfo + interest + taxes) / interest
  original: Cash interest coverage = (CFO + interest paid + taxes paid) / interest paid
  source_id=bsec-draft-ratios locator=Printed p61, Annexure C, Section F cash-flow ratios 7, 9 and 11
  translation: Inputs and numerator are same-currency flows; positive interest is a payment magnitude. CFO may be negative.
  var cfo          role=input   qty=cash_flow_from_operating_activities                     unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: cash flow from operating activities
  var interest     role=input   qty=interest_paid_in_operating_cash_flow                    unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: interest paid
  var taxes        role=input   qty=taxes_paid_in_operating_cash_flow                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: taxes paid
  var ratio        role=output  qty=cash_interest_coverage_ratio                            unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Pre-interest and pre-tax operating cash flow relative to cash interest paid
  guards: ['interest > 0', 'taxes >= 0']
  assumptions: ['Interest and taxes added back were included in the stated operating cash flow.', 'All flows cover the same entity, currency and period.']
  limitations: ['When interest paid was classified as financing rather than operating, do not add it back; that alternative requires a different numerator.', 'This cash-based definition is not the EBIT interest-coverage ratio.']

financial-analysis--valuation-operating-cash-flow-per-share@r1 :: Operating cash flow per weighted-average share
  kind=definition expr=result = amount / shares
  original: Operating cash flow per weighted-average share = cash flow from operations / weighted average ordinary shares
  source_id=bsec-draft-ratios locator=Printed p60, Annexure C, Section E valuation ratios 2, 3 and 7
  translation: Share count is dimensionless. Currency output represents currency per one ordinary share.
  var amount       role=input   qty=cash_flow_from_operating_activities                     unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: cash flow from operations
  var shares       role=input   qty=weighted_average_common_shares                          unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Positive weighted-average ordinary shares in the period
  var result       role=output  qty=operating_cash_flow_per_weighted_share                  unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: cash flow from operations per ordinary share

financial-analysis--units-of-production-depreciation@r1 :: Units-of-production depreciation expense
  kind=definition expr=expense = (cost - residual) * produced / capacity
  original: Depreciation = (cost - residual value) × period production / estimated lifetime production
  source_id=openstax-depreciation locator=Table 11.2 and units-of-production example
  translation: Physical output units are identical counts, and their quotient is dimensionless; the result is a currency amount for the reporting period.
  var cost         role=input   qty=depreciable_asset_cost                                  unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: asset cost
  var residual     role=input   qty=asset_estimated_residual_value                          unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: estimated residual value
  var produced     role=input   qty=period_production_units                                 unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Units produced in the specified period
  var capacity     role=input   qty=estimated_lifetime_production_units                     unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Positive estimated total lifetime production units
  var expense      role=output  qty=units_of_production_depreciation_expense                unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: depreciation expense
  guards: ['cost >= residual', 'residual >= 0', 'produced >= 0', 'capacity > 0', 'produced <= capacity']
  assumptions: ['Production and capacity use the same unit, asset and estimated lifetime.', 'The remaining depreciable amount is sufficient for the calculated charge.']
  limitations: ['Changes in estimates, impairment, residual-floor adjustments and accumulated production beyond capacity require separate handling.']

financial-analysis--asset-taxable-temporary-difference-deferred-liability@r1 :: Deferred tax liability from an asset taxable temporary difference
  kind=definition expr=liability = (carrying - taxbase) * rate
  original: Deferred tax liability = (asset carrying amount - tax base) × applicable tax rate
  source_id=ias12-2021 locator=Paragraphs 15, 24–25 and 47; examples of taxable and deductible differences
  translation: This is an undiscounted liability from one asset; the positive taxable difference is carrying amount less tax base.
  var carrying     role=input   qty=asset_carrying_amount                                   unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: asset carrying amount
  var taxbase      role=input   qty=asset_tax_base                                          unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: asset tax base
  var rate         role=input   qty=deferred_tax_reversal_rate                              unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Enacted or substantively enacted rate applicable when the difference reverses
  var liability    role=output  qty=asset_deferred_tax_liability                            unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: deferred tax liability
  guards: ['carrying >= taxbase', 'rate >= 0', 'rate <= 1']
  assumptions: ['The temporary difference is taxable on recovery and recognized under the applicable standard; no recognition exception applies.', 'A single enacted or substantively enacted rate applies to the expected recovery.']
  limitations: ['Different tax rates, recovery methods, recognition exceptions and offset rules require separate analysis.', 'Tax effects booked in OCI or equity still require appropriate balance-sheet recognition and allocation.']
  unresolved: []

financial-analysis--deferred-tax-liability-rate-change-effect@r1 :: Deferred tax liability change from a rate change
  kind=definition expr=change = difference * (new - old)
  original: Change in deferred tax liability = temporary difference × (new tax rate - old tax rate)
  source_id=ias12-2021 locator=Paragraphs 6, 15, 24–25, 47 and 60–62
  translation: Rates are base-one fractions; the sign of output is the change in the liability.
  var difference   role=input   qty=taxable_temporary_difference                            unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar

  var new          role=input   qty=new_deferred_tax_rate                                   unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: New enacted or substantively enacted rate for the reversal period
  var old          role=input   qty=old_deferred_tax_rate                                   unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Previously applicable rate for the reversal period
  var change       role=output  qty=deferred_tax_liability_rate_change                      unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: change in deferred tax liability
  guards: ['difference >= 0', 'new >= 0', 'new <= 1', 'old >= 0', 'old <= 1']
  assumptions: ['The same recognized taxable temporary difference is held fixed for the rate-change comparison.', 'Each rate is enacted or substantively enacted and applicable to the expected reversal.']
  limitations: ['This isolates the rate effect and excludes temporary-difference changes, exchange effects and recognition changes.', 'Presentation in profit or loss versus OCI or equity depends on the underlying transaction.']

  original: Enterprise value = equity market value + debt market value - cash

      def: market value of equity

      def: market value of debt

      def: cash
  var enterprise   role=output  qty=enterprise_value_equity_debt_cash                       unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: enterprise value
  guards: ['equity >= 0', 'debt >= 0', 'cash >= 0']

financial-analysis--operating-break-even-unit-volume@r2 :: Operating break-even volume in units
  kind=definition expr=units = fixed / contribution
  original: Break-even unit volume = fixed costs / contribution per unit
  source_id=openstax-managerial-3-2 locator=Section 3.2, break-even point in units
  translation: A unit is treated as a dimensionless count, so the contribution input is currency per one unit.
  var fixed        role=input   qty=fixed_operating_costs                                   unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: period fixed operating costs
  var contribution role=input   qty=contribution_margin_per_unit                            unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: contribution margin per unit
  var units        role=output  qty=operating_break_even_units                              unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: Number of units whose contribution equals fixed operating costs
  guards: ['fixed >= 0', 'contribution > 0']
  assumptions: ['Fixed costs are for the same activity period as the unit volume; unit contribution is positive and constant.', 'Sales mix, price and variable cost per unit remain constant over the relevant range.']
  limitations: ['This is a continuous accounting threshold; if units are indivisible, round up externally.', 'This excludes income taxes, financing costs and fixed-cost step changes.']
  unresolved: []

financial-analysis--after-tax-return-on-average-invested-capital@r1 :: After-tax return on average invested capital
  kind=definition expr=roic = profit / capital
  original: ROIC = after-tax operating profit / average book invested capital
  source_id=valmont-2025-roic locator=Selected Financial Measures, Return on Invested Capital, printed pp30–31
  translation: Currency cancels; the output is a base-one return for the stated reporting period.
  var profit       role=input   qty=after_tax_operating_profit                              unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: after-tax operating profit before financing cost
  var capital      role=input   qty=average_book_invested_capital                           unit=USD      dims=currency      domain=Finite real scalar
      def: average book value of invested capital
  var roic         role=output  qty=after_tax_return_on_invested_capital                    unit=1        dims=dimensionless domain=Finite real scalar
      def: After-tax operating profit divided by average book invested capital
